  <div class="kp-phase" id="kp-phase-te-delegation">
    <div class="kp-nav-row">
      <button class="kp-back" onclick="kpGoBack('team-hub')">
        <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M19 12H5M12 5l-7 7 7 7"/></svg>
        All exercises
      </button>
      <div class="kp-nav-row-sep"></div>
      <button class="kp-home-link" onclick="kpGoHome()">
        <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M3 9l9-7 9 7v11a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2z"/><polyline points="9 22 9 12 15 12 15 22"/></svg>
        Learning Lab
      </button>
    </div>
    <div class="kp-phase-label">Team Exercise — Delegation and Team Roles</div>
    <h2 style="font-size:clamp(20px,2.5vw,28px);font-weight:800;color:var(--navy);letter-spacing:-.02em;line-height:1.1;margin:0 0 32px;max-width:720px">Who’s Responsible for What?</h2>


  <div class="te-print-bar">
    <p>Key points are hidden by default &mdash; expand them on screen, or print with or without them.</p>
    <button class="te-print-btn" onclick="printVersion('participant')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; questions only
    </button>
    <button class="te-print-btn primary" onclick="printVersion('facilitator')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; with key points
    </button>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head">The scenario</div>
  <div class="te-case-intro">
    <p>Dr. Lavoie is the Qualified Investigator on a Phase II oncology drug trial at the RI-MUHC. Her research team includes:</p>
    <div class="te-roster">
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Marie</strong>Senior CRC, 8 years of experience. Managing this study since activation. Registered nurse.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Kevin</strong>Research Assistant, joined 3 months ago. Previously in a data entry role.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Dr. Ou&eacute;draogo</strong>Sub-investigator, cardiologist. Listed on the delegation log.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>MUHC Phlebotomy Team</strong>Core lab team handling all blood draws. Not currently on the delegation log.</div>
    </div>
    <p>Dr. Lavoie travels frequently and is sometimes unreachable for 2&ndash;3 days at a time. The study is currently enrolling.</p>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 1</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>A potential participant came in for a screening visit while Dr. Lavoie was at a conference in Vancouver. Marie reviewed the inclusion/exclusion criteria, determined the participant met all criteria, and recorded in the source document: <em>&ldquo;Patient meets all eligibility criteria. Consent obtained.&rdquo;</em> Marie is listed on the delegation log for &ldquo;screening and eligibility review.&rdquo; She conducted the consent discussion and obtained the participant&rsquo;s signature.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Is there a delegation or responsibility issue here? What is the specific concern, and what should have happened instead?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Two issues. First, <strong>&ldquo;screening and eligibility review&rdquo; may or may not cover consent</strong> &mdash; it depends on how the task is worded on the delegation log. Consent is a distinct, regulated activity, not automatically covered by a screening entry.</p>
          <p>Second, the eligibility determination (&ldquo;meets all criteria&rdquo;) is a clinical judgment that should involve physician review, particularly for an oncology trial where criteria require interpretation of clinical findings.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.1, &sect;2.3.2, &sect;2.8.5 &middot; GUI-0100 C.05.010(f)(g)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Marie is a registered nurse with 8 years of experience. Does her experience and qualification change whether this was appropriate? Where is the line between being <em>qualified</em> to do something and being <em>authorized</em> to do it?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Experience and competence are necessary but not sufficient. GCP requires both <strong>qualification</strong> and <strong>documented delegation</strong> for the specific task. A nurse with 8 years of experience who is not delegated for consent is not authorized to obtain consent. The delegation log is the mechanism through which the PI exercises oversight &mdash; it is not a formality.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.2</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    
      
    </ul>
  </div>



  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 2</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Kevin noticed a participant&rsquo;s platelet count was borderline for one of the exclusion criteria &mdash; just above the minimum threshold. He discussed it briefly with Marie, who said &ldquo;it&rsquo;s fine, just record it.&rdquo; Kevin entered the lab value and noted &ldquo;within eligibility range&rdquo; in the CRF.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
<li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Kevin was told &ldquo;it&rsquo;s fine&rdquo; by a more senior team member. What is Kevin&rsquo;s personal responsibility here from a GCP perspective? What should he have done?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Kevin recorded a clinical judgment (&ldquo;within eligibility range&rdquo;) outside his delegated authority as an RA. <strong>Marie&rsquo;s verbal instruction does not constitute delegation</strong> &mdash; delegation requires a documented entry on the log. &ldquo;A senior person told me it was fine&rdquo; is not a defence in an audit. Kevin should have escalated the borderline value to the QI or sub-investigator for a documented physician decision.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.2 &middot; C.05.010(f)(g)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 3</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>The phlebotomy team draws blood for PK sampling at protocol-specified timepoints with specific timing and handling requirements. They have never been added to the delegation log. The study has been running for 11 months.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Under ICH E6(R3), does the phlebotomy team need to be on the delegation log at all? If the group decides they should be added now, how should that be handled &mdash; and what are the risks of adding them retroactively?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.3 introduced a proportionality principle: <strong>individuals performing activities as part of routine clinical practice no longer need to be on the delegation log</strong>. The question is whether protocol-specific PK draws at defined timepoints qualify as &ldquo;routine.&rdquo; Groups often disagree on this &mdash; that disagreement is the point of the exercise.</p>
          <p>If the group decides they should be added: the entry must be transparently dated with the actual date of addition, never backdated to study start. Retroactive documentation is a data integrity risk even when the correction is legitimate.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.3 &middot; GUI-0100 (manager delegation provision)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 4</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Dr. Lavoie returned from the conference and signed the eligibility checklists for three participants enrolled during her absence, dating her signature as the day she returned.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Is this a data integrity issue, a delegation issue, both, or neither? What would a monitor think when they see a cluster of documents all signed on the same day?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Both. The signatures imply contemporaneous review &mdash; but the PI was not at the site during those enrollments. <strong>A cluster of documents all signed on the same return date is a common monitor and inspector red flag.</strong> The problem is not that the PI reviewed the documents; it is that the date misrepresents when the review occurred.</p>
          <p>Dr. Ou&eacute;draogo should have conducted and signed the eligibility assessments during the absence, if he was delegated for that task. Retrospective PI signatures are one of the most commonly observed findings in academic site audits.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) GCP Principles &sect;10 &middot; ALCOA+ (contemporaneous)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Reflection</div>
  <div class="te-reflection">Looking back at the four situations: is there anything here that reflects something you&rsquo;ve seen, done, or been asked to do in your own work? What would you do differently now?</div>

  <div class="te-sources"><strong>Sources:</strong> ICH E6(R3) (effective April 1, 2026) &middot; GUI-0100 (updated March 2026) &middot; Health Canada Division 5 C.05.010, C.05.012 &middot; [SOP-CR-002](/sops/cr-002) v.09 &middot; Civil Code of Quebec</div>

  </div>

  
  <div class="kp-phase" id="kp-phase-te-consent">
    <div class="kp-nav-row">
      <button class="kp-back" onclick="kpGoBack('team-hub')">
        <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M19 12H5M12 5l-7 7 7 7"/></svg>
        All exercises
      </button>
      <div class="kp-nav-row-sep"></div>
      <button class="kp-home-link" onclick="kpGoHome()">
        <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M3 9l9-7 9 7v11a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2z"/><polyline points="9 22 9 12 15 12 15 22"/></svg>
        Learning Lab
      </button>
    </div>
    <div class="kp-phase-label">Team Exercise — Informed Consent Process</div>
    <h2 style="font-size:clamp(20px,2.5vw,28px);font-weight:800;color:var(--navy);letter-spacing:-.02em;line-height:1.1;margin:0 0 32px;max-width:720px">Did We Actually Consent This Participant?</h2>


  <div class="te-print-bar">
    <p>Key points are hidden by default &mdash; expand them on screen, or print with or without them.</p>
    <button class="te-print-btn" onclick="printVersion('participant')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; questions only
    </button>
    <button class="te-print-btn primary" onclick="printVersion('facilitator')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; with key points
    </button>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head">The scenario</div>
  <div class="te-case-intro">
    <p>Dr. Chen is a neurologist and PI on a Phase III trial studying a new treatment for mild-to-moderate Alzheimer&rsquo;s disease. The study involves quarterly cognitive assessments, blood draws, one lumbar puncture at baseline, and two optional MRI scans. The trial has been running for 14 months.</p>
    <div class="te-roster">
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Dr. Chen</strong>Neurologist. PI on the study and also the treating physician for several enrolled participants.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Andr&eacute;</strong>Experienced CRC. Has been managing the study since activation.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Mrs. Beaumont</strong>74 years old. Patient of Dr. Chen&rsquo;s. Enrolled with a diagnosis of mild Alzheimer&rsquo;s disease. First language: French.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Mr. Tremblay</strong>Enrolled participant. Has completed 3 of 4 quarterly assessments.</div>
    </div>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 1</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Mrs. Beaumont came in for her initial consent visit. Her daughter had brought her after reading about the study online. During the consent discussion, Mrs. Beaumont seemed uncertain &mdash; she kept looking at her daughter and asking &ldquo;is this a good idea?&rdquo; Her daughter answered &ldquo;yes, Mom, it&rsquo;s the best thing for you.&rdquo; Andr&eacute; completed the consent discussion and obtained Mrs. Beaumont&rsquo;s signature. He noted in the source record: <em>&ldquo;Patient appeared nervous but signed without further questions.&rdquo;</em></p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">What are the concerns with how this consent was obtained? Is &ldquo;patient appeared nervous but signed&rdquo; adequate documentation of a valid consent process?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Two concerns. First, <strong>undue influence</strong>: a family member who is answering consent questions on behalf of the participant and saying &ldquo;it&rsquo;s the best thing for you&rdquo; is exercising influence over the decision. TCPS2 is explicit that consent must be free and voluntary. The discussion should have been conducted with Mrs. Beaumont independently, or with explicit documentation that she was making the decision herself.</p>
          <p>Second, <strong>therapeutic misconception</strong>: Dr. Chen is both the treating neurologist and the PI. Mrs. Beaumont may be consenting because she trusts her doctor, not because she genuinely understands the research context. This dual role should be explicitly addressed during the consent discussion.</p>
          <p><strong>&ldquo;Patient appeared nervous but signed&rdquo; is not documentation of valid consent</strong> &mdash; it raises more questions than it answers. Documentation should record: information provided, time offered, questions asked and answered, comprehension assessment, and that the decision was voluntary and without coercion.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">TCPS2 Art. 3.1, 3.7 &middot; ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.8.1, &sect;2.8.3 &middot; Civil Code Art. 20</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 2</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Three months into the study, the sponsor issued a protocol amendment adding a second lumbar puncture at month 12. The sponsor advised that re-consent was only required &ldquo;for participants who have not yet reached month 12.&rdquo; Andr&eacute; has 6 enrolled participants: 2 are past month 12 and had their lumbar puncture before the amendment was issued. He re-consented the other 4. The 2 who were past month 12 were told about the change verbally but did not sign a new consent form.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Is the sponsor&rsquo;s position correct? What are the obligations toward the 2 participants who were past month 12 when the amendment was issued? Does it matter that they already had the lumbar puncture?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The sponsor&rsquo;s position is <strong>incomplete</strong>. The obligation to re-consent is triggered by new information material to participant welfare or consent &mdash; not only by whether the participant will undergo the new procedure.</p>
          <p>For the 2 participants past month 12: they will not have the second lumbar puncture, but they are still participating in a study whose protocol has changed. TCPS2 requires that researchers promptly inform all affected participants &mdash; including those who won&rsquo;t undergo the new procedure &mdash; when new information is relevant to their ongoing participation. Verbal notification without documentation is not sufficient.</p>
          <p>Whether formal re-consent (with signature) is required for participants who won&rsquo;t undergo the added procedure is ultimately a judgment for the REB &mdash; but the site should not accept the sponsor&rsquo;s direction on re-consent without confirming it with the REB.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">TCPS2 Art. 3.1, 11.7, 11.8 &middot; ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.9</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 3</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>At month 6, Mrs. Beaumont scores significantly worse on the cognitive assessment &mdash; her condition has progressed. During a follow-up visit, she seems confused about why she is at the hospital. When Andr&eacute; asks if she has any questions about the study, she says &ldquo;I don&rsquo;t really know what this study is anymore.&rdquo; Her daughter is present and says &ldquo;she&rsquo;s having a bad day &mdash; she still wants to be in the study.&rdquo;</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">What should Andr&eacute; do now? What is the legal framework in Quebec for this situation? Does the daughter&rsquo;s statement that Mrs. Beaumont &ldquo;still wants to be in the study&rdquo; resolve the issue?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Mrs. Beaumont&rsquo;s statement &ldquo;I don&rsquo;t really know what this study is anymore&rdquo; is a significant indicator of capacity loss for the purpose of research consent. TCPS2 Article 3.7 requires that capacity be reassessed when there is reason to believe it has changed &mdash; this is one of those moments.</p>
          <p>Under Civil Code <strong>Article 21</strong>: if Mrs. Beaumont is now incapable of consenting to research, her continued participation requires proxy consent from her mandatary or tutor &mdash; not simply family agreement. <strong>The daughter&rsquo;s presence does not automatically make her the legal proxy.</strong> Is there a mandatary designated? Does the protocol have a defined procedure for this transition? The REB should have reviewed this scenario.</p>
          <p>Also note Civil Code <strong>Article 24</strong>: even with valid proxy consent, if Mrs. Beaumont objects to continued participation, her objection overrides the proxy&rsquo;s consent. The team should not assume that family willingness equals participant willingness.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">Civil Code Art. 21, 24 &middot; TCPS2 Art. 3.7 &middot; ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.8.1</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 4</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Mr. Tremblay calls Andr&eacute; and says he wants to withdraw from the study. He has completed 3 of the 4 quarterly assessments and his data to date is complete. Andr&eacute; thanks him, processes the withdrawal, and removes his file from the active participant list. The sponsor subsequently asks for access to Mr. Tremblay&rsquo;s previously collected data for the final analysis.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Can the sponsor now use Mr. Tremblay&rsquo;s previously collected data? What should Andr&eacute; have discussed with Mr. Tremblay before processing the withdrawal?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The answer depends on what the consent form said and what Mr. Tremblay said when he withdrew &mdash; which is precisely the problem. <strong>Andr&eacute; processed the withdrawal without having the data conversation.</strong></p>
          <p>When a participant withdraws, the site should discuss: (a) whether the participant is withdrawing from procedures only, or also requesting withdrawal of previously collected data; (b) what the consent form says about data use on withdrawal; (c) any limitations on data withdrawal once data has been analyzed or submitted.</p>
          <p>ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.9.1 specifically asks investigators to consider requesting continued consent for use of already-collected data. TCPS2 Art. 3.1 states participants can request data withdrawal subject to feasibility. <strong>Treating withdrawal as a form-completion exercise rather than a consent conversation about data</strong> is one of the most common consent management failures.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">TCPS2 Art. 3.1, 3.3 &middot; ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.9.1 &middot; Loi 25 (data subject rights)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Reflection</div>
  <div class="te-reflection">Looking across all four situations: consent is treated as an event in each one &mdash; get the signature, file it, move on. What would it look like at your site to treat consent as an ongoing process instead? What would change?</div>

  <div class="te-sources"><strong>Sources:</strong> TCPS2 (2022), Chapters 3 and 11 &middot; Civil Code of Quebec, Articles 20, 21, 24 &middot; ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.8&ndash;2.9 (effective April 1, 2026) &middot; Health Canada Division 5, C.05.010(h) &middot; Loi 25 / EFVP framework &middot; [SOP-CR-008](/sops/cr-008) v.08</div>
  </div>

  
  <div class="kp-phase" id="kp-phase-te-sae">
    <div class="kp-nav-row">
      <button class="kp-back" onclick="kpGoBack('team-hub')">
        <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M19 12H5M12 5l-7 7 7 7"/></svg>
        All exercises
      </button>
      <div class="kp-nav-row-sep"></div>
      <button class="kp-home-link" onclick="kpGoHome()">
        <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M3 9l9-7 9 7v11a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2z"/><polyline points="9 22 9 12 15 12 15 22"/></svg>
        Learning Lab
      </button>
    </div>
    <div class="kp-phase-label">Team Exercise — SAE and Adverse Event Reporting</div>
    <h2 style="font-size:clamp(20px,2.5vw,28px);font-weight:800;color:var(--navy);letter-spacing:-.02em;line-height:1.1;margin:0 0 32px;max-width:720px">The Friday Afternoon Cascade</h2>


  <div class="te-print-bar">
    <p>Key points are hidden by default &mdash; expand them on screen, or print with or without them.</p>
    <button class="te-print-btn" onclick="printVersion('participant')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; questions only
    </button>
    <button class="te-print-btn primary" onclick="printVersion('facilitator')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; with key points
    </button>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head">The scenario</div>
  <div class="te-case-intro">
    <p>It is <strong>Friday at 4:45 PM</strong>. Dr. Ndiaye is the QI on a Phase II oncology immunotherapy trial. She is at a medical conference in Toronto and is not responding to pages.</p>
    <div class="te-roster">
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Dr. Ndiaye</strong>QI. At a conference in Toronto. Not reachable.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Sophie</strong>CRC. Delegated for SAE documentation and source record completion.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Dr. Beaumont</strong>Sub-investigator. Delegated for medical assessments.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Liam</strong>RA. Study coordinator for data management.</div>
    </div>
    <p>This case is a cascade &mdash; each situation compounds the previous one. Work through the questions in order.</p>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 1</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p><strong>4:45 PM Friday:</strong> Sophie receives a voicemail from a participant&rsquo;s spouse. Mr. Bouchard (enrolled 6 weeks ago) was admitted to hospital <em>yesterday morning</em> with Grade 3 pneumonitis and has been in the ICU since then.</p>
      <p>Sophie checks the Investigator&rsquo;s Brochure. The current version (v5.2) lists pneumonitis as a known adverse reaction, Grade 1&ndash;2 only. Grade 3 pneumonitis is <strong>not listed</strong>.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">What is the site awareness date for this SAE, and why does it matter? What should Sophie&rsquo;s first action be after listening to the voicemail?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The site awareness date is <strong>Friday</strong> &mdash; the date Sophie listened to the voicemail. &ldquo;Site&rdquo; includes all delegated study staff; Sophie&rsquo;s awareness equals the site&rsquo;s awareness. She cannot use the excuse that the PI didn&rsquo;t know until Monday.</p>
          <p>The voicemail states the hospitalization started &ldquo;yesterday morning&rdquo; &mdash; that is the event onset date. But the <strong>reporting clock runs from site awareness</strong>, not event onset.</p>
          <p>First action: <strong>document the awareness contemporaneously</strong> &mdash; write a dated, timed note in the source record or study log recording the call: who called, what they said, what time Sophie listened to the voicemail. Then contact Dr. Beaumont (delegated for medical assessments) for causality assessment.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.7.2 &middot; C.05.014 &middot; ALCOA+ (contemporaneous)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 2</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Sophie contacts Dr. Beaumont. Dr. Beaumont reviews the case by phone and assesses the event as &ldquo;possibly related.&rdquo; She tells Sophie: <em>&ldquo;Fill in the SAE form and I&rsquo;ll sign it Monday when I&rsquo;m in.&rdquo;</em></p>
      <p>Meanwhile, Liam suggests: <em>&ldquo;The protocol says planned hospitalizations aren&rsquo;t SAEs &mdash; can we call this a disease progression event?&rdquo;</em></p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Dr. Beaumont assessed the event by phone and will sign the form Monday. What are the problems with this arrangement? Is it acceptable for an SAE assessment to be verbal and documented days later?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>This is a common workaround that creates problems. The 24-hour sponsor notification clock (typical protocol requirement) runs from <strong>site awareness on Friday</strong>. If no form is submitted until Monday, the site may already be in violation regardless of whether the form is &ldquo;complete.&rdquo;</p>
          <p>Better approach: Sophie should <strong>initiate an SAE form Friday</strong> with available information, note the verbal assessment from Dr. Beaumont, mark it as preliminary pending physician signature, and submit it to the sponsor within the protocol timeline. Dr. Beaumont then signs the formal version Monday &mdash; dated Monday.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.7.2 &middot; [SOP-CR-012](/sops/cr-012) &middot; Protocol-specific timelines</span>
        </div>
      </li>
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Liam suggests the &ldquo;planned hospitalization&rdquo; exemption might apply. Evaluate this. Does it apply here? How should the team handle uncertainty about whether an exemption applies?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Mr. Bouchard was admitted with Grade 3 pneumonitis &mdash; an acute, unexpected respiratory complication. This is <strong>not a planned hospitalization</strong> and is not disease progression. Liam&rsquo;s suggestion reflects a real temptation: find a reason not to report.</p>
          <p>The rule when uncertain: <strong>report</strong>. The sponsor can determine if a protocol exemption applies. Over-reporting carries far lower regulatory risk than under-reporting a SUSAR.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E2A &middot; C.05.014 &middot; [SOP-CR-012](/sops/cr-012)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 3</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Saturday morning: Sophie reviews the SAE form. She writes the site awareness date as <strong>Monday</strong> &mdash; when she will be at the office and has access to all the documentation.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Sophie writes Monday as the awareness date. What is the consequence if discovered in an audit? How should she have documented the Friday awareness?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Writing Monday as the awareness date when Sophie became aware on Friday is <strong>falsification of a regulatory record</strong> &mdash; one of the most serious data integrity violations in clinical research. Inspectors routinely check call logs, voicemail records, and email timestamps against SAE awareness dates.</p>
          <p>Sophie should document: <strong>site awareness date &mdash; Friday [date]</strong>. The form can note: &ldquo;Site learned of event via voicemail at 4:48 PM on [Friday]. PI unreachable; sub-investigator Dr. Beaumont contacted by phone same day for preliminary assessment. SAE form initiated [Saturday or Monday] and submitted [date]. Awareness date and form completion date differ.&rdquo; Both dates should be accurate and the reason for the gap explained.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ALCOA+ (contemporaneous, accurate) &middot; C.05.012 &middot; ICH E6(R3) data integrity principles</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Situation 4</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What happened</div>
      <p>Monday: the sponsor&rsquo;s monitor calls. Three other sites have reported Grade 3 pneumonitis in the past two weeks. The sponsor&rsquo;s medical monitor now believes this is a SUSAR. They ask Sophie: <em>&ldquo;Can you change Dr. Beaumont&rsquo;s assessment to &lsquo;probably related&rsquo; to help us with our Health Canada filing?&rdquo;</em></p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">What should Sophie do? What can the sponsor legitimately ask, and what crosses the line?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Sophie must not change Dr. Beaumont&rsquo;s assessment simply because the sponsor asks. <strong>Causality assessment is the investigator&rsquo;s independent clinical judgment.</strong> A sponsor cannot instruct a site to change it.</p>
          <p>What the sponsor <strong>can</strong> do: provide the new safety information (the 3 other sites&rsquo; cases) and ask the QI or sub-investigator to review and reconsider. If Dr. Beaumont reviews the new data and independently changes her assessment, that is acceptable &mdash; with documentation of her clinical reasoning.</p>
          <p>What the sponsor <strong>cannot</strong> do: instruct the site to change the assessment to match their preferred determination, or have a CRC change it without physician involvement.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.7.2 &middot; ICH E2A &middot; Data integrity principles</span>
        </div>
      </li>
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">The sponsor now believes this is a SUSAR. Walk through the three conditions required for SUSAR classification. Does this event qualify? What does SUSAR classification mean for the site&rsquo;s obligations?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Three conditions: <strong>serious</strong> (ICU hospitalization, life-threatening) ✓ + <strong>unexpected</strong> (Grade 3 pneumonitis not in IB v5.2) ✓ + <strong>related</strong> (Dr. Beaumont: possibly related) ✓ = SUSAR.</p>
          <p>Site obligations once a SUSAR is identified: the sponsor reports to Health Canada within 7 days (fatal/life-threatening) or 15 days (other). The <strong>site must notify the local REB</strong> &mdash; this is a separate parallel obligation, not handled by the sponsor. The site must also ensure currently enrolled participants are informed if the SUSAR materially affects their consent, and may need to reassess other participants showing early pneumonitis signs.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">C.05.001 (SUSAR definition) &middot; C.05.014 &middot; TCPS2 Art. 11.7&ndash;11.9 &middot; ICH E2A</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Reflection</div>
  <div class="te-reflection">Each decision in this case made the next problem worse. Looking at your own site: what systems or habits would have changed the outcome at each step?</div>

  <div class="te-sources"><strong>Sources:</strong> ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.7.2 (effective April 1, 2026) &middot; ICH E2A (adverse event definitions and reporting) &middot; Health Canada Division 5 C.05.001, C.05.014 &middot; [SOP-CR-012](/sops/cr-012) v.08 &middot; TCPS2 (2022) Art. 11.7&ndash;11.9 &middot; ALCOA+ data integrity principles</div>
  </div>

  
  <div class="kp-phase" id="kp-phase-te-deviations">
    <div class="kp-nav-row">
      <button class="kp-back" onclick="kpGoBack('team-hub')">
        <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M19 12H5M12 5l-7 7 7 7"/></svg>
        All exercises
      </button>
      <div class="kp-nav-row-sep"></div>
      <button class="kp-home-link" onclick="kpGoHome()">
        <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M3 9l9-7 9 7v11a2 2 0 0 1-2 2H5a2 2 0 0 1-2-2z"/><polyline points="9 22 9 12 15 12 15 22"/></svg>
        Learning Lab
      </button>
    </div>
    <div class="kp-phase-label">Team Exercise — Protocol Deviations</div>
    <h2 style="font-size:clamp(20px,2.5vw,28px);font-weight:800;color:var(--navy);letter-spacing:-.02em;line-height:1.1;margin:0 0 32px;max-width:720px">How Bad Is It?</h2>


  <div class="te-print-bar">
    <p>Key points are hidden by default &mdash; expand them on screen, or print with or without them.</p>
    <button class="te-print-btn" onclick="printVersion('participant')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; questions only
    </button>
    <button class="te-print-btn primary" onclick="printVersion('facilitator')">
      <svg width="12" height="12" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><polyline points="6 9 6 2 18 2 18 9"/><path d="M6 18H4a2 2 0 0 1-2-2v-5a2 2 0 0 1 2-2h16a2 2 0 0 1 2 2v5a2 2 0 0 1-2 2h-2"/><rect x="6" y="14" width="12" height="8"/></svg>
      Print &mdash; with key points
    </button>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head">The scenario</div>
  <div class="te-case-intro">
    <p>The RI-MUHC research team is conducting a Phase III trial of an oral medication for treatment-resistant hypertension. The CRC, Am&eacute;lie, has just completed a routine file review covering the past two months and has flagged five issues for discussion.</p>
    <div class="te-protocol-box">
      <strong>Key protocol requirements</strong>
      <ul style="margin:10px 0 0 18px;padding:0">
        <li><strong>Inclusion:</strong> SBP 160&ndash;180 mmHg at screening, confirmed by two measurements 10 minutes apart</li>
        <li><strong>Primary endpoint:</strong> Change in SBP from baseline at Week 12</li>
        <li><strong>Required visits:</strong> Baseline, Week 4, Week 8, Week 12 (&plusmn;7 days)</li>
        <li><strong>Adherence:</strong> IP taken daily; &ge;80% required for per-protocol analysis</li>
        <li><strong>Exclusion:</strong> ACE inhibitors</li>
      </ul>
    </div>
    <p>Work through each issue: classify it, assess its severity, and identify what actions are required. One of the five issues may not be a deviation at all &mdash; check your calculations.</p>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Issue 1 &mdash; PT-003</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What was found</div>
      <p>PT-003 had SBP readings of <strong>178 and 159 mmHg</strong> at screening. The second reading was below the 160 mmHg minimum. The site coordinator at the time noted <em>&ldquo;borderline &mdash; enrolled.&rdquo;</em> PT-003 has now been in the study for 8 weeks.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Is this a protocol deviation? If so, is it important or non-important? What actions are now required, and in what order?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p><strong>Important protocol deviation</strong> &mdash; eligibility deviation. The second SBP reading of 159 mmHg is below the 160 mmHg minimum. The participant does not meet the inclusion criterion. This affects data integrity (their baseline SBP may not match the target population) and potentially participant safety.</p>
          <p>Required actions in order: (1) document the deviation today with today&rsquo;s date; (2) have the PI assess impact on PT-003&rsquo;s safety; (3) notify the sponsor immediately; (4) notify the MUHC REB; (5) conduct root cause analysis &mdash; why was a borderline reading allowed to stand?; (6) develop a substantive CAPA; (7) determine with the sponsor what happens to PT-003&rsquo;s data (likely excluded from per-protocol analysis).</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.5.2 &middot; C.05.010(a) &middot; MUHC REB policies</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Issue 2 &mdash; PT-007</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What was found</div>
      <p>PT-007 completed the Week 8 visit on <strong>Day 61</strong>. Am&eacute;lie also notes the Week 12 visit was completed on <strong>Day 79</strong>. She is not sure whether either visit is outside the protocol window and has flagged both for review.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">Are either of these visits outside the protocol window? The protocol specifies Week 8 = Day 56 &plusmn;7 days and Week 12 = Day 84 &plusmn;7 days. Check the calculation before answering.</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p><strong>Neither visit is a deviation.</strong> Week 8 window: Day 56 &plusmn;7 = Days 49&ndash;63. Day 61 is within window. Week 12 window: Day 84 &plusmn;7 = Days 77&ndash;91. Day 79 is within window.</p>
          <p>Am&eacute;lie&rsquo;s uncertainty about the calculation is itself a quality signal &mdash; CRCs should be confident in visit window arithmetic. More importantly: <strong>a non-deviation that gets documented as a deviation creates unnecessary regulatory noise</strong> and suggests a site that doesn&rsquo;t understand its own protocol. Verify before you document.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.5.2 &middot; Protocol-specific visit windows</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Issue 3 &mdash; PT-011</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What was found</div>
      <p>PT-011 reported at the Week 4 visit that they started taking <strong>ramipril</strong> (an ACE inhibitor &mdash; a protocol exclusion criterion) two weeks after enrollment, prescribed by their family physician for a different indication. PT-011 has been on ramipril for 6 weeks.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">This is a protocol deviation and a potential safety concern. How do these two obligations interact? Should the team address the safety concern before reporting the deviation, or do both proceed simultaneously?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p><strong>Important protocol deviation</strong> &mdash; exclusion criterion violation. A participant is on a prohibited concomitant medication. This affects both safety (ACE inhibitor interaction with study IP on blood pressure) and data integrity (ACE inhibitor as confound on the primary endpoint).</p>
          <p>The deviation report and the safety assessment run <strong>simultaneously</strong> &mdash; not sequentially. The team should not wait to fully document the safety picture before reporting the deviation. The PI should assess whether the combination poses a risk to PT-011 and whether AE monitoring or SAE reporting is triggered. Both tracks proceed in parallel.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.5.2, &sect;2.7.2 &middot; C.05.010 &middot; [SOP-CR-012](/sops/cr-012)</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Issue 4 &mdash; PT-014</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What was found</div>
      <p>PT-014&rsquo;s Week 4 pill count showed <strong>68% adherence</strong> (protocol requires &ge;80%). The CRC at the time noted this in the source record but did not report it as a deviation. PT-014 is now at Week 8 with 71% adherence.</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">The finding was documented but not reported as a deviation. What is the impact of an undocumented or under-reported deviation on the study data, on an audit, and on the team&rsquo;s credibility?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The adherence threshold crossed a protocol-defined boundary &mdash; whether this triggers an important deviation depends on the protocol&rsquo;s classification criteria. But that determination should have been made explicitly and documented. A source record note is not a deviation report.</p>
          <p>Impact of under-reporting: (a) <strong>data</strong> &mdash; the deviation cannot be assessed for impact if it isn&rsquo;t formally documented; (b) <strong>audit</strong> &mdash; &ldquo;if it&rsquo;s not documented, it didn&rsquo;t happen&rdquo; applies to deviations too. An inspector finding a protocol threshold crossed in source records with no deviation report will treat it as a quality system failure; (c) <strong>credibility</strong> &mdash; &ldquo;we noticed it but didn&rsquo;t think it was important enough to report&rdquo; is one of the most problematic things a site can say in an inspection.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.5.2 &middot; CAPA principles &middot; GCP documentation principles</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Issue 5 &mdash; PI signatures</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <div class="te-situation">
      <div class="te-situation-eyebrow">What was found</div>
      <p>The PI signed all four screening eligibility checklists on the same day, <strong>two weeks after</strong> the participants enrolled. The checklists show his signature as &ldquo;reviewed and approved.&rdquo;</p>
    </div>
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question">
        <div class="te-q-text">This issue appeared in the Delegation exercise as a data integrity concern. How does it also manifest as a protocol deviation concern? And what is the connection to Issue 1 &mdash; PT-003&rsquo;s eligibility deviation?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>If the protocol requires PI review and approval of eligibility <em>before</em> enrollment, the PI signing two weeks later is a deviation from that specific protocol requirement &mdash; not just a data integrity issue. The signature implies contemporaneous review but the date shows it wasn&rsquo;t.</p>
          <p>The connection to Issue 1: <strong>if PT-003 had received contemporaneous PI review, the 159 mmHg reading might have been caught before enrollment</strong>. The retrospective signature practice didn&rsquo;t just create a documentation problem &mdash; it removed a quality checkpoint that was supposed to prevent eligibility errors.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.3.1, &sect;2.5.2 &middot; ALCOA+ (contemporaneous) &middot; Protocol-specific PI oversight requirements</span>
        </div>
      </li>
    </ul>
  </div>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Reflection</div>
  <div class="te-reflection">Five issues in two months across eligibility, concomitant medications, adherence, and PI oversight. Does this look like an isolated bad-luck period, or a systemic quality problem? What would a root cause analysis need to ask?</div>

  <div class="te-sources"><strong>Sources:</strong> ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.5.2 (effective April 1, 2026) &middot; Health Canada Division 5 C.05.010 &middot; CAPA principles &middot; GCP documentation and data integrity principles</div>
  </div>

      <button class="kp-back" onclick="kpGoBack('team-hub')">

  <div class="te-section-head">The scenario</div>
  <div class="te-case-intro">
    <p>The RI-MUHC research team has just received a Health Canada inspection notice for <strong>Study CARD-2023-008</strong>, a Phase III cardiovascular drug trial. The inspection will take place in <strong>12 business days</strong>. It is the morning after receiving the notice.</p>
    <div class="te-roster">
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Dr. Patel</strong>QI. Has been away frequently; some eligibility checklists were signed in batches.</div>
      <div class="te-roster-item"><strong>Am&eacute;lie</strong>CRC. Has been managing the study for 22 months with 18 enrolled participants.</div>
    </div>
    <div class="te-status-label" style="margin-top:20px;font-size:9px;font-weight:700;letter-spacing:.14em;text-transform:uppercase;color:var(--textm)">What Am&eacute;lie knows going into the meeting</div>
    <div class="te-status-box">
      <ul>
        <li>5 protocol deviations documented &mdash; including the two PT-003 eligibility entries and the PT-011 concomitant medication issue</li>
        <li>Two SUSARs reported from other sites globally; sponsor distributed SUSAR notices to all investigators</li>
        <li>Last monitoring visit was 6 months ago; <strong>4 CRF queries are still open</strong></li>
        <li>Current IB is version 5.2; the ISF contains <strong>version 5.0</strong></li>
        <li>Some eligibility checklists were <strong>signed by Dr. Patel in batches</strong>, retrospectively</li>
        <li>Monitor access to OACIS has never been formally set up &mdash; the last CRA reviewed printouts instead</li>
      </ul>
    </div>
  </div>

  
  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Preparing for day one</div>

        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Inspectors typically begin with: the <strong>ISF</strong> (organized, complete, current?); the <strong>delegation log</strong>; the <strong>QIU form</strong> (QI registered with Health Canada?); one or two <strong>consent forms</strong> for version verification; the most recent <strong>monitoring visit report</strong> (what has the sponsor already found?).</p>
          <p>Given what Am&eacute;lie knows, the highest-risk areas are: the IB version discrepancy (v5.0 in ISF vs. current v5.2); the retrospective PI signatures; the 4 unresolved queries; and the PT-003 eligibility deviation misclassified as non-important despite protocol language requiring important classification.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) Appendix C &middot; C.05.012 &middot; GUI-0100</span>

        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">

          <p><strong>Do NOT:</strong> backdate any documents; reclassify deviations retroactively without documented rationale; alter source records or consent forms; prepare &ldquo;clean&rdquo; copies of working documents.       <li class="te-question" style="border-top:none">
        <div class="te-q-text">Four CRF queries have been open for 6 months. How significant is this? What does it signal to an inspector about site quality? If the team resolves them now, what should they be prepared to explain?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>Four open queries from 6 months ago signals poor query management, possible CRC workload issues, weak sponsor-site communication, and potentially unresolved data quality issues. To an inspector this is a quality system concern, not just an administrative oversight.</p>
          <p>The team should resolve them before the inspection if possible &mdash; but the resolution dates will be visible in the audit trail and will show they were closed immediately pre-inspection. <strong>Have an explanation ready</strong> for why these took so long to close. &ldquo;We&rsquo;ve been busy&rdquo; is not sufficient.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.12 &middot; C.05.012</span>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">The IB version chain</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question" style="border-top:none">
        <div class="te-q-text">The IB in the ISF is v5.0; the current version is v5.2. This connects directly to the SAE reporting exercise. Walk through the full chain of consequences. What does the CAPA for this need to address?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The chain: v5.0 in ISF &rarr; staff assessing AE expectedness against v5.0 &rarr; PT-019&rsquo;s Grade 3 pneumonitis classified as &ldquo;expected&rdquo; against v5.0 &rarr; not classified as SUSAR &rarr; expedited reporting timeline not triggered &rarr; potential Division 5 violation.</p>
          <p>The CAPA must address three things: (1) <strong>IB receipt, filing, and version control procedures</strong> &mdash; who is responsible for filing IB updates and communicating them to the team; (2) <strong>retrospective review</strong> of any SAE/AE assessments made against v5.0 that should have been made against v5.2; (3) <strong>whether any events need to be reclassified and re-reported</strong> to the sponsor and REB.</p>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Dr. Patel&rsquo;s role</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <ul class="te-questions">

        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p><strong>Dr. Patel should:</strong> be present for the opening and closing meetings; be available for questions about clinical decisions (causality assessments, eligibility decisions, emergency situations); review and understand the deviation log before the inspection; know the key issues and the agreed responses.</p>
          <p><strong>He should NOT:</strong> speak on behalf of staff about processes he wasn&rsquo;t personally involved in; improvise answers to questions he doesn&rsquo;t know; become defensive about observations; sign anything during the inspection that he hasn&rsquo;t reviewed.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">ICH E6(R3) &sect;2.1, &sect;2.3 &middot; C.05.010(f) &middot; GUI-0100</span>
        </div>

    </ul>

  <div class="te-section-head" style="margin-top:40px">Contesting an observation</div>
  <div class="te-block">
    <ul class="te-questions">
      <li class="te-question" style="border-top:none">
        <div class="te-q-text">At the closing meeting, the inspector notes two apparently retroactive delegation log entries as a Risk 2 (major) observation. Dr. Patel disagrees &mdash; he says the entries were made on time but dated incorrectly due to a typing error. How should this be handled at the closing meeting and in the CAPA response?</div>
        <button class="te-kp-toggle" onclick="teToggle(this)">Key points <svg width="14" height="14" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2.5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"><path d="M6 9l6 6 6-6"/></svg></button>
        <div class="te-kp-body">
          <p>The closing meeting is the right time to raise a disagreement. Dr. Patel should: politely explain the discrepancy (typing error, not retroactive entry); offer to provide supporting evidence (any contemporaneous correspondence showing the delegation was in place at the earlier date); and ask the inspector to note the site&rsquo;s position in the draft exit notice.</p>
          <p>The inspector may maintain the observation pending review of the evidence. The formal CAPA response is another opportunity to provide evidence and request a risk reclassification. <strong>The site should not simply accept all observations without response if they have evidence to the contrary.</strong> Unchallenged observations become part of the inspection record and affect future inspection risk scoring.</p>
          <span class="te-kp-ref">Health Canada inspection process &middot; GUI-0100 &middot; ALCOA+ (accurate)</span>

    </ul>
